## Status icons — the canonical definitions

These six are the ENTIRE vocabulary. `scripts/uat-audit.py` fails the build on any
verdict glyph outside this set, because an uncounted glyph is silently dropped by
`uat-backfill.py` and then reads downstream as "never walked" — which turns an
unreadable verdict into a missing pass. Four rows carried a stray `◑` that way, and
`⏳` dropped thirteen more, before the guard existed.

| icon | name | means | what moves it |
|---|---|---|---|
| `✅` | PASS | Every clause in the test case was measured on the CURRENT env and held, with a control showing the check could have come out the other way. | Nothing — until the env is replaced or the clause changes, when it returns to ☐. |
| `❌` | FAIL | Measured on the current env and it did NOT hold. A named, reproducible defect. This is the honest home for anything undelivered — a feature that has not shipped is a FAIL, not a block. | A fix, deployed, then re-walked. |
| `⚠️` | PARTIAL | **Working state only — never a terminal verdict.** Some clauses proven, others not. A test case whose clause does not fully hold is a FAILING test case, so a row may not be REPORTED in this state: resolve it to ✅ or ❌ before a convergence cycle is captured. It exists so a walk-in-progress can be saved mid-flight. | Closing the named residual (→ ✅) or accepting it as the failure it is (→ ❌). |
| `⛔` | SUPERSEDED | The test case cannot be answered as written — its subject no longer exists, or it asserts a value the schema forbids. **Not** a parking space for hard work. | Retire-and-REPLACE, one-for-one, recorded in `uat-retirements.csv`. |
| `☐` | NOTRUN | Never walked on this env. Includes a freshly replaced clause, which must NOT inherit the retired row's verdict. | Walking it. |
| `⏳` | PENDING | **Working state only — never a terminal verdict**, same rule as PARTIAL. Written by `scripts/reset-uat.py` when a new env flushes prior evidence, to mark awaiting-re-walk as distinct from never-walked. A row may not be REPORTED in this state: it is outside the PASS/FAIL vocabulary a reader counts, so a row left here is invisible rather than merely undecided — 3 rows were uncounted that way. | Re-walking on the live env, then ✅ or ❌. |

**The denominator is STONE at 286.** Retiring a row always carries a replacement so
the count never moves; `scripts/uat-snapshot.py` refuses any other total. A
floating denominator makes every percentage incomparable across cycles, and it has
drifted before.

**Evidence is per-env.** A new Sovereign flushes ALL prior evidence, so the score
resets to near-zero on every fresh prov (measured: 133 green 2026-07-30 → 25 on
2026-08-03). A drop across an env boundary is the ledger working; a drop WITHIN one
env is a regression. `scripts/uat-drift-guard.py` refuses a ✅ row that cites a
wiped env.

**Env:** live omantel.biz Sovereign, dep `91dc05917e44d1c1`, 2-region converged (region-a 62/62 HRs · region-b 56/56 HRs, me-east-215-a + me-east-215-b). All verdicts below are against THIS env unless a row carries its own labeled prior-env evidence.

**The single decisive gate:** kom4dc EIP quota is **10** (free 3); a fresh post-fix 2-region prov needs **6** EIPs — a HARD WALL with zero orphans to reclaim. Raising the quota **10 → ≥16** would unblock the 7 roll-gated tickets in one fresh prov. The remaining 6 are Anthropic-credential-walled (2), a real remaining build (1), or destructive-walk-gated (2, need a throwaway env [itself EIP-walled] or founder clearance).

The obvious read of 'region-A carries console-https-uatco, region-B does not' is 'the org-controller runs single-region and never writes region-B'. That is WRONG, and this row's own text contains the counter-evidence: region-B's cilium-gateway-console carries 8 listeners INCLUDING per-Org ones for *.r17probe.omani.homes. A controller that could not reach region-B could not have written those either. So region-B IS reachable and HAS been written; what it holds is a per-Org listener set from an EARLIER Org that was never updated for uatco and never cleaned up for r17probe. Anyone fixing this by 'making the controller multi-region' would be fixing a mechanism that is already there. WHAT THE SOURCE SHOWS. tenant_console_tls.go appends the console-https-<slug>/console-http-<slug> pair idempotently to the SHARED kube-system/cilium-gateway-console; teardown IS wired - teardownTenantNetworking (tenant_networking_teardown.go:112) is called from organization_controller.go:455 and is held open by the dedicated TenantNetworkingFinalizer precisely so the cascade cannot be skipped when no other finalizer is present (#4459). So neither the write nor the delete path is missing code. The r17probe listener surviving an Org whose API record reads state='deleted' is therefore NOT a teardown-logic bug - it is #5426 (Organization delete never runs the finalizer cascade: no REST route, no UI action, so the CR is never deleted and the finalizer never fires). The listener outlives the Org because nothing ever asked it to die. CLASSIFICATION: gap (a) belongs to the #5635 family (single-region resource behind a two-region shared EIP, ~50% fresh-TCP failure) - same mechanism, applied to the console listener instead of the app namespace - and the orphan half is #5426. Not filed as a new issue; a third ticket for a mechanism twice-filed would inflate the backlog without adding a fix. NOTE THE MEASUREMENT DISCIPLINE THIS ROW ALREADY GOT RIGHT and that made the adjudication possible: fresh-TCP curl with --no-keepalive. Under HTTP/2 the browser pins one backend and the same host reads 10/10, which is how a 50% split hides. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 PLATFORM REGRESSION, DATED. Same dated cause as row 87 (#5246). The door-collapse claim itself still holds — one catalyst-ui door, BSS a path on it; what fails is 'lands 200', at 5/10. ‖ NO-PARTIALS DISPOSITION 2026-08-10 — RECLASSIFIED ⚠️ -> ❌. Every clause in a test case must hold for the row to pass; this row's evidence NAMES a clause that does not. A partial is a FAILING test case with some passing parts, and carrying it as its own class let real failures sit in a comfort bucket where no backlog counted them — 27 rows were doing exactly that. NOTHING WAS RE-MEASURED HERE and no verdict got worse: the residual named above is the same residual, in the same words, with the same evidence. Only the class changed, from one that reads as 'nearly there' to one that reads as 'does not pass'. This moves ZERO rows out of green, so it is neither progress nor regression — it is the removal of a hiding place. The row closes when the named residual closes. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: ENV-STATE — needs an environment SHAPE this Sovereign does not have (pre-cutover window, single region, or a destructive prov->wipe cycle). Not a code gap and not re-walkable here.|

What SURVIVES is the gateway surface, and it survives in BOTH regions: region-A 1 orphaned HTTPRoute; region-B 1 orphaned HTTPRoute PLUS 2 orphaned Gateway listeners still carrying the deleted Org's hostname. ROOT CAUSE, which the same-day #5635 work makes exact: CREATION now happens through TWO producers across TWO regions (catalyst-api's multi-region emitter and the org-controller's single-region one), while TEARDOWN targets only the org-controller's route NAME and runs in only ONE cluster. CLEAN: namespace `r17probe` ABSENT in both regions; Organization CR **0** (control: the same probe lists the two live Orgs `hw292-omani-works` + `uatco`, so this 0 is a real absence — an earlier pass of mine queried the wrong API group `organizations.catalyst.openova.io` and returned empty, which would have read as clean vacuously; the correct group is `organizations.orgs.openova.io`); HelmReleases 0/0; Certificates 0/0; DNSEndpoints **0/0 — the DNS-leak clause of this row PASSES**. LEAKED, so the row stays ❌: (1) HTTPRoute `catalyst-system/catalyst-ui-r17probe-omani-homes` → `console.r17probe.omani.homes` orphaned in **BOTH** regions; (2) Gateway listeners `console-https-r17probe` + `console-http-r17probe` on `kube-system/cilium-gateway-console` host `*.r17probe.omani.homes` orphaned in **region-b only**; (3) TLS Secret `kube-system/org-wildcard-tls-r17probe-omani-homes` orphaned in **region-a only** — a wildcard certificate for a deleted Organization still sitting in kube-system. So the cascade reaps namespace, Organization CR, HelmReleases, Certificates and DNS correctly, and misses the ingress surface plus one TLS Secret. The region-b/region-a asymmetry is #5649 + #5359 (region-b never receives the reap because it reconciles from public github.com). Evidence: docs/sessions/2026-08-04/perregion-and-dns-walks/README.md. Deployed catalyst-api on hw292: fad88bd. `git merge-base --is-ancestor` -> NO for both commits, so the running binary provably predates the reaper. No chart change was needed for the local-cluster half, so the deferral rested on a condition that was already satisfied. (2) SHIPPED: teardownTenantRoute reaped exactly ONE route, by a name derived from the Org's own hostname (catalyst-ui-<host-dashed>), which finds only the route the org-controller itself created. It now ALSO reaps by the label every producer stamps on create (openova.io/organization=<slug>, tenant_route.go:144) — broader and more stable than adding a second hard-coded name, since a third producer is covered automatically. (3) STILL BROKEN, which is why the verdict does not move: the reaper runs in ONE cluster. CONSEQUENCE: a deleted Org keeps a live HTTPRoute claiming console.<slug>.omani.homes and a TLS secret for it — a DNS/route leak that would collide with any future Org re-using that subdomain, which is exactly the failure mode this row exists to catch. Stays ❌ with the reap sites now named: the org delete-cascade must also reap catalyst-system HTTPRoutes and kube-system org-wildcard-tls secrets keyed on the slug. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: ENV-STATE — needs an environment SHAPE this Sovereign does not have (pre-cutover window, single region, or a destructive prov->wipe cycle). No PIN prompt, no email form, no /login redirect. Same caveat as row 26 (this row is its duplicate assertion): a "Sign in" button still renders in the banner DOM alongside the authenticated chip — the #5123-B session-scope display class also seen on the BSS billing page (row 72). Landing PASSES; the literal no-login-form sub-clause does not  ‖ THE SIGN-IN-BUTTON RESIDUAL IS FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5825, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292), and it was the SAME defect in rows 1, 26 and 72 - one cause, three rows. ProfileMenu decided 'signed in' from useSession ALONE (the cookie session off GET /whoami). The sovereign console can also be authenticated by the legacy OIDC PKCE token set, where /whoami answers 401 and session.signedIn is false. SovereignSidebar ALREADY falls back to the id_token claims for exactly this reason (#4187, its own comment says so) - so the two identity readers in the SAME header disagreed: the sidebar painted 'EB emrah.baysal@openova.io' while ProfileMenu painted a [Sign in] button three inches away. That is precisely what this row recorded. NOT COSMETIC: the row's clause is 'lands /dashboard signed-in as the owner (no PIN/login form)', and a visible sign-in affordance IS a login form by that clause's own definition - which is why the row sat at partial rather than green. FIX: ProfileMenu applies the sidebar's fallback chain with the sidebar's precedence - cookie authoritative, id_token fallback - so one resolved identity drives the avatar, its title and the dropdown and the two readers cannot name different people. EXPIRY IS CHECKED, not just presence: a stale token set outlives the session in sessionStorage, and accepting it would trade a false 'signed out' for a false 'signed in', the worse direction, because it fronts an avatar for a session that can no longer call the API. Uses isTokenExpired, the same 60s-margin helper the OIDC refresh path uses, so it cannot drift from that layer. sessionStorage access is wrapped - privacy mode throws and the header must never crash over an optional fallback. 5 guards, and 4 mutations each turned a DIFFERENT one red with no overlap: session-only revert, dropped expiry check, sub-as-email fallback, precedence flip. The storage-key constants are duplicated in the fixture, which is a real hazard - a typo makes loadTokens() return null and every 'rejects' assertion pass for the wrong reason - so the POSITIVE test is what keeps them honest. Gates: tsc rc=0, 4 auth+layout suites 24 tests green. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 VERDICT PROMOTED FROM ⚠️ — deploy-gate CONFIRMED by two-way ancestry. Ground: the clause demanded a SERVER redirect; core/marketplace is a STATIC build whose auth is deliberately client-side, so there is no SSR redirect to observe and the assertion contradicts the shipped contract rather than testing it. NOT deleted — the slot is reused so the denominator stays frozen at 286, and the full swap is in docs/ledger/uat-retirements.csv. Reset to ☐ because a NEW clause has never been walked; inheriting the old row's verdict would be the fabrication this ledger exists to stop. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 WALKED ON THE REPLACEMENT CLAUSE - FAILS, and the mechanism is architectural rather than a bug in the page. A live unredeemed voucher was taken from GET /api/v1/org/billing/vouchers/list (26 available, plan m, 50 OMR) and its redeem URL opened in the SAME browser that held the authed owner session. RESULT: the browser stayed on marketplace/redeem/ - redirectCount 0, no bounce to /dashboard - and the STRANGER flow rendered: 1 form, 1 voucher-code input, a 'Sign up to redeem' button, and the voucher-valid copy. The owner identity appears nowhere on the page. SESSION CONTROL, which is what makes this a finding instead of an expired cookie: navigating straight back to the console seconds later returned GET /api/v1/whoami = 200 with email emrah.baysal@openova.io and tier `owner`. The session was live the whole time. MECHANISM, measured not argued: document.cookie is EMPTY on the console's OWN origin, so the catalyst_session cookie is HttpOnly. The marketplace is a separate host running a STATIC build with CLIENT-side auth, and client-side JS cannot read an HttpOnly cookie - so a static marketplace page has no readable signal that the visitor is the owner, on this or any other Sovereign. The owner bounce cannot happen through that path BY CONSTRUCTION, not by oversight. This is the same conclusion the retired clause reached from the opposite direction: the retired version demanded a SERVER redirect, which a static build cannot perform; the replacement demanded a CLIENT bounce, which an HttpOnly session cannot trigger. Both fail for the same underlying reason, so this is a real product gap and not a mis-stated assertion: an owner opening a redeem link is shown a signup form. TO CLOSE: the marketplace needs a readable owner signal - a non-HttpOnly hint cookie scoped to the parent domain, or a whoami call from the marketplace origin with CORS credentials - and the row then asserts the bounce against that. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: NEEDS-CODE — no fix exists yet. This row is engineering work, not a walk; a re-walk cannot change it.|

ROOT CAUSE, corrected from the ticket: #5434 diagnosed this as 'the backend never inverts the producer's parameters.databases[].consumer map'. That is not what was wrong. The inverter ALREADY EXISTED and was correct — dependsOnFromHRGraph reads the HelmRelease's Flux dependsOn graph and resolves each target's declared Context entry — but it ran ONLY inside synthesiseAppFromHelmRelease, which is gated on the Application CR being ABSENT. A bootstrap app like spine-harbor HAS a CR (spec {bootstrap:true, helmRelease:{name:bp-harbor, namespace:flux-system}}), so it took HandleApplicationGet, found no spec.dependsOn, and returned empty while the working resolver sat one branch away. The fix is a fallback that reaches the existing resolver, NOT a second derivation: inverting the producer map was declined deliberately because two ways to compute one edge is how the CR path and the HR path drifted apart into this bug in the first place. Guarded by an AST test on the CALL SITE rather than the helper — the defect was never a wrong resolver, it was a resolver nothing called, which is the shape a behaviour test cannot see. Mutation-verified: deleting the call site, removing the delegation, and stripping the rationale comment each turn a test RED. Five of the seven fields are enum vocabulary where that reads fine; two are IDENTIFIERS, and the slug is the join key an operator types into kubectl and the API - a value you cannot copy off the screen is not canonical, which is precisely what this row asserts. FIX: capitalisation is now OPT-IN per call site (enumValue prop); Slug and Owner do not set it. The DIRECTION of the default is the actual fix - had capitalisation stayed default, the next field added to this <dl> would silently inherit the mangling, which is exactly how this shipped. An un-title-cased enum is a cosmetic miss; a mangled identifier is wrong data. GUARD, both directions mutation-verified: restoring the blanket capitalize turns the identifier test RED, stripping capitalize from every field turns the CONTROL test RED. The assertion is on the CLASS, not the text, deliberately - text-transform changes only what is PAINTED, textContent stays 'uatco' either way and jsdom does no layout, so a textContent assertion would be the archetypal guard that cannot go red (identical before and after the fix). textContent is still asserted alongside for a DIFFERENT failure: dropping the class invites someone to reach for toLowerCase() in JS, which the class check alone would miss. COUNT clause still PARTIAL, and the hw291 discrepancy has moved: the feed no longer disagrees with the CRs - GET /api/v1/sovereigns/1c56518035a83e03/applications returns exactly 14 items and kubectl get applications -A returns exactly the same 14 (spine-gitea, spine-harbor, spine-keycloak, spine-openbao, guacamole, harbor, uat-ahs-pg, newapi, shared-pg, shared-pg-b, shared-pg-c, uatco-agenity, uatco-mail, uatco-openclaw), so hw291's 10-installed-vs-8-CRs gap is GONE. What remains is that the GRID is not keyed on Application at all: all 46 cards are bp-* blueprint slots, and not one of the 14 Application CRs renders as its own card - shared-pg, harbor, spine-gitea and the three uatco apps are absent as cards even though they are the objects the row counts. So it is neither one-card-per-Application nor one-per-HelmRelease; it is one-per-blueprint-slot. Consistent with row 188: catalyst-api likewise has no card.  ROOT CAUSE SHARPENED 2026-08-07 (source read, no env needed) — the residual is ARCHITECTURAL, not a count bug. The apps grid does not render the Application ESTATE at all; it renders the blueprint CATALOG. AppsPage.tsx:116 builds its card list from `resolveApplications(store.selectedComponents)`, and applicationCatalog.ts:72 iterates BOOTSTRAP_KIT plus the selected component set — i.e. the wizard store's catalog. Application CRs never contribute card IDENTITY. They are layered in only as STATUS: AppsPage.tsx:130-134 comments it exactly — 'Layer in live status from /api/v1/sovereign/apps so the AppCards reflect ground truth'. So the walk's observation (46 bp-* cards, 14 Application CRs, none of them rendering as their own card) is not a discrepancy between two counts; it is one collection being displayed and a different collection being consulted for badges. CONSEQUENCE: this row's assertion ('one card per Application') and the implementation disagree at the design level, so no small patch satisfies it. Making it true means re-keying the grid on the Application list and treating catalog entries as the not-yet-installed remainder — a deliberate UX change, not a bugfix. THIS NEEDS ADJUDICATION, NOT A UNILATERAL REWRITE: /apps currently answers 'what CAN this Sovereign run', and the row asks it to answer 'what IS running'. Both are legitimate products; only the founder should choose. I am deliberately not 'fixing' this by rewriting the grid, because that would silently change an operator surface to satisfy a ledger row. | 25 | model | [#3687](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/3687) | One consistent model across surfaces: `/organizations` shows the Orgs that `/apps`, the dashboard and showback all agree on. | hw292-2026-08-10 | ❌ | hw292-2026-08-06T01:55Z RE-WALK (dep 1c56518035a83e03, authed owner, four surfaces sampled in one pass): IMPROVED on one residual, two persist, two new. RESOLVED - hw291 residual (c): the apps feed and the CRs now agree exactly, 14 == 14 (see row 19), so the installed-vs-CR gap is closed. PERSISTS - residual (a) in altered form: GET /api/v1/org/consumption labels the parent group 'hw292.omani.works' (isParent:true, costUnits 0) while the directory and the Organization CR both say 'hw292-omani-works' - the literal word 'sovereign' is gone but showback and the directory still do not use the same identifier for the same Org. PERSISTS - residual (b) partially: the treemap Organization layer renders 'Platform overhead' + 'uatco' but the PARENT Org hw292-omani-works has no node, while showback does emit it (at 0 units). NEW (i): the /apps grid agrees with neither - it shows 0 of the 14 Applications and 46 blueprint slots instead, so 'the Orgs that /apps agrees on' cannot be evaluated from that surface at all. NEW (ii): GET /api/v1/organizations returns 3 records where only 2 Organization CRs exist - the extra is r17probe with state:'deleted' and no CR, and /organizations still renders a link to it. Soft-deleted rather than the #5395 orphan class, but the directory and the CR set still do not match 1:1.  ‖ RESIDUAL (a) FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5819, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292), AND IT ALSO EXPLAINS RESIDUAL (b). consumptionParentOrg returned the RAW Sovereign FQDN, so showback labelled the parent group 'hw292.omani.works' while the Organization CR, the directory and the treemap all use the slug form 'hw292-omani-works' (spineOrganizationSlug -> orgNamespace). Two spellings of one Org is exactly what this row asserts against. Note this row already recorded it as PERSISTING: an earlier fix removed the literal word 'sovereign' from this path without touching the mismatch underneath, which is why it survived. IT WAS NEVER ONLY COSMETIC. The parent bucket is selected by `org == parentOrg`, where org comes from the openova.io/organization NAMESPACE LABEL, and label values written by the org-controller are CR slugs - DNS-1123, dots impossible. An FQDN-shaped parentOrg could therefore never match anything, so the parent group rendered at 0 cost units on a Sovereign plainly running workloads. That is residual (b): this row's own observation that showback emits the parent at 0 while the treemap Organization layer has no parent node. ONE root cause, TWO symptoms - they were recorded as separate residuals. FIX: route through orgNamespace(), the canonical org->identifier seam (namespace_ensure.go), the same one the self-org CR producer uses. The 'sovereign' fallback for an unresolvable deployment is kept deliberately - it is VISIBLY synthetic, and inventing a plausible-LOOKING slug for an Org we cannot identify would read as a real Organization on the panel. 5 guards, mutation-verified: reverting to the FQDN turns 3 RED; re-deriving inline as strings.ReplaceAll(fqdn,'.','-') turns 2 RED; deleting the reasoning comment turns 1 RED. The AGREEMENT test is the load-bearing one - it compares against spineOrganizationSlug, the producer, NOT a hand-written literal, because a literal lets the two drift the moment either derivation changes. The SEAM test is why the inline mutation exists: ReplaceAll gets hw292.omani.works right and passes a naive behaviour test, then diverges on orgNamespace's dash-run collapse (it fails on a--b..c.example), which is precisely how the two derivations drifted apart to begin with. Gates: go vet clean, full handler suite 255s green. NOT CLAIMED: residual NEW (i) - the /apps grid showing 0 of 14 Applications - is #5814. Residual NEW (ii) - GET /api/v1/organizations returning 3 records against 2 Organization CRs - is untouched and still open. ‖ RESIDUAL (ii) FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5833, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292). '3 records where only 2 Organization CRs exist' is mergeOrgResponses keying on Subdomain ALONE. Two holes, both producing a DUPLICATE rather than a miss - which is why the symptom was an inflated count and not a disappearing Org: (1) the CR side ALWAYS sets Subdomain to the slug (orgCRToResponse) while the store side sets whatever the provision record carries, and a custom-domain Org (DomainMode != free-subdomain) legitimately has NO pool subdomain, so one Organization gets two different keys; (2) an EMPTY key skipped dedupe ENTIRELY - both loops appended without registering anything, so a store row with no subdomain and its own CR row both landed. FIX: two rows are the same Org when they match on EITHER axis (OrganizationID or Subdomain), both registered on insert, axes namespaced id:/sub: so a subdomain equal to another Org's tenant id cannot merge two DIFFERENT Organizations. A row identifiable on neither axis is KEPT but explicitly excluded from dedupe rather than silently treated as deduped. THE CORRECTION WORTH CARRYING: my first cut keyed on OrganizationID with Subdomain as fallback. ALL SIX TARGETED TESTS PASSED. The FULL suite then went red on a pre-existing case - a store record carrying uuid-acme and the CR for the same slug acme have DIFFERENT ids, so id-first split one Organization into two, mirroring the very defect being fixed. Six green targeted tests are not evidence that an invariant holds, only evidence about the six things they check; the 254s full suite is what caught it. That invariant is now pinned by TestMergeOrgResponses_SameSlugDifferentIDsIsOneOrg. 7 guards, mutation-verified 4 ways, plus controls that a too-AGGRESSIVE key would drop the funnel-only rows #4479 added this merge to surface. Gates: go vet clean, full handler suite 254s green. STILL OPEN ON THIS ROW: residual (a) identifier mismatch is #5819 (merged), NEW (i) /apps showing 0 of 14 Applications is #5814 (merged) - both deploy-gated; the treemap parent-node half rides on #5819's join repair. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 VERDICT PROMOTED FROM ⚠️ — deploy-gate CONFIRMED by two-way ancestry. The cited fix is verifiably NOT an ancestor of the deployed image, and the CONTROL runs the identical command against a commit that IS one (`<deployed>^` → ancestor, exit 0) and one that is not (origin/main HEAD → exit 1), so the check has been seen to answer both ways. This is a determinate state, not a partial: no source work remains and it closes on the next prov. hw292 cannot receive it — the cutover chart wants 0.1.174, has 0.1.159 applied, and cannot upgrade because the upgrade needs the registry path that is itself broken. Clauses (a),(b),(ii) are deploy-gated; clause (i) inherits row 15's producer wall below and will NOT close on a roll. ‖ NO-PARTIALS DISPOSITION 2026-08-10 — RECLASSIFIED ⚠️ -> ❌. Every clause in a test case must hold for the row to pass; this row's evidence NAMES a clause that does not. A partial is a FAILING test case with some passing parts, and carrying it as its own class let real failures sit in a comfort bucket where no backlog counted them — 27 rows were doing exactly that. NOTHING WAS RE-MEASURED HERE and no verdict got worse: the residual named above is the same residual, in the same words, with the same evidence. Only the class changed, from one that reads as 'nearly there' to one that reads as 'does not pass'. This moves ZERO rows out of green, so it is neither progress nor regression — it is the removal of a hiding place. The row closes when the named residual closes. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: DEPLOY-GATED — the fix is MERGED and simply not running on this env. | 29 | sso | [#3374](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/3374) | Re-open the bare console URL after the session TTL → lands signed-in again, no PIN re-prompt. | hw292-2026-08-09 | ❌ | hw292-2026-08-08 LIVE BROWSER WALK (Playwright). CORRECTS AN EARLIER STAMP OF MINE FROM THE SAME DAY. I first recorded that the console does NOT silently re-authenticate from a live keycloak realm session. THAT IS WRONG - the mechanism works, and my test never invoked it. RE-RUN, identical except one step: signed in via PIN, traversed grafana to prime the realm session, dropped ONLY catalyst_session (keeping all four realm cookies AUTH_SESSION_ID, KC_AUTH_SESSION_HASH, KEYCLOAK_IDENTITY, KEYCLOAK_SESSION), CLEARED sessionStorage, reloaded the bare console URL -> lands on /dashboard with ZERO email inputs, signed in as the owner, with freshly minted oidc access/id/refresh tokens. WHAT THE FIRST RUN MEASURED: I had not cleared sessionStorage, so catalyst:authed=1 survived from the initial login while the cookie was gone. router.tsx calls this the 'stale-marker fast path' and notes the layout 'discovers the 401 when the stale-marker fast path has bypassed this gate' - so the app took the fast path, hit a 401 and dropped to the PIN wall WITHOUT attempting silent SSO. I saw the PIN wall and blamed the mechanism; the mechanism never ran. THE ROW STILL FAILS, for a narrower and better reason: a genuine TTL expiry produces exactly the state my first run created by accident - the cookie expires on the server clock while catalyst:authed=1 persists for the tab's lifetime - so a real re-entry hits the fast path, 401s and PIN-walls, while the silent path that demonstrably works never fires. The defect is 'when catalyst:authed is stale relative to the session cookie, the fast path bypasses silent SSO instead of falling back to it', tracked on #5887. Evidence docs/sessions/2026-08-08/hw292-ttl/hw292-silent-reauth-works.png. ‖ hw292-2026-08-08 — FIX WRITTEN AND MERGED THIS SESSION (#5909), delivery blocked by the same source-level freeze as the rest. Root cause established earlier today: router.tsx:287 'if (hasCatalystSession()) return' short-circuits BEFORE line 290's probeWhoamiAndCacheMarker, so #5460's 401-revocation was unreachable from the one gate that needed it — the stale marker was self-perpetuating at the only site that could clear it, and the #3374 Layer-B silent-SSO leg was skipped with it. #5909 gives the marker a written-at stamp that ages out after 5 min; missing, non-numeric and future stamps all read stale, and all four production write sites now go through markAuthed() so no call site can mint an un-ageing marker. Gates at merge: 62 auth-gate tests, 229 files / 2121 tests full UI suite, tsc clean. WHY IT IS STILL ❌: the console is delivered by bp-catalyst-platform, which reads Ready=False DependencyNotReady — inside the 51-HelmRelease cascade rooted at bp-cilium SourceNotReady, 'no auth config for ghcr.io in the docker-registry Secret ghcr-pull' (#5759). Source-correct and unwalked; resolves on the fresh prov, not on more code. This completes the ❌ sweep: all 14 failing rows now carry a 2026-08-08 walk. ‖ hw292-2026-08-09 BROWSER WALK, HALF PROVEN — STAYS ❌. Playwright, fresh context, no session: GET https://console.hw292.omani.works/ redirects to /login and PAINTS a real PIN wall — h1 'Sign in', 'Enter your email to receive a 6-digit PIN', the origin echoed back, an email textbox, and a Send code button that is DISABLED until an address is entered. No dashboard shell, no stale signed-in state. Screenshot row29-console-unauth-login.png. WHY THIS IS NOT GREEN: the defect #5909 fixed is hasCatalystSession() returning true off a marker with NO catalyst:authed-at stamp, short-circuiting the whoami probe at router.tsx:287. A FRESH browser never had a marker, so this walk cannot separate 'the staleness check works' from 'the staleness check never had to run' — it is vacuous against the actual clause. Closing it needs a stale marker INJECTED into sessionStorage (key catalyst:authed='1' with catalyst:authed-at absent) followed by a reload, asserting the PIN wall still wins. Refusing to stamp green on a control that cannot fail. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: ENV-STATE — needs an environment SHAPE this Sovereign does not have (pre-cutover window, single region, or a destructive prov->wipe cycle). Not a code gap and not re-walkable here.|

| 37 | sso | [#3374](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/3374) [#3858](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/3858) [#4136](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/4136) | newapi bare URL (1st hit) → lands on `/console` signed-in as admin (role 100); no "Unknown OAuth provider", no login page. | hw292-2026-08-08 | ❌ |hw292-2026-08-03T22:10-22:22Z LIVE BROWSER WALK (Playwright). never reaches /console; direct API navigation to /api/user/self returns 'Unauthorized, not logged in and no access token provided' so there is no admin and no role 100. The 'Unknown OAuth provider' string is ABSENT. 4/4. ROOT CAUSE, common to 26/29/32/34/36/37/38: the console session is the root of the entire SSO tree on this env — catalyst-api is the catalyst-pin OIDC provider (CATALYST_OIDC_PROVIDER_ENABLED=true, CATALYST_PIN_ISSUER=https://console.hw292.omani.works), so with no console session every service needing a FRESH handshake collapses onto the PIN form. Grafana/gitea/openbao/hubble pass only because they hold their own long-lived post-handshake sessions. Live contributing fault CONFIRMED by me directly, not taken from the walker: catalyst-api-655f6457c6-b9mbd lastState.terminated.reason=OOMKilled exit=137 at 2026-08-03T22:10:28Z with restartCount=15, requests.memory=96Mi against limits.memory=4Gi — the #4811 class. CONTROL: the identical query against catalyst-ui returns reason=Unknown restarts=1, so the probe discriminates. NOT claimed: that the OOM caused the lost session — the session cookie is signed/stateless from a stable Secret and would survive a restart. Every failure was 100%% deterministic across 3-5 fresh loads, so this is NOT the #5635 per-region split. hw292-2026-08-06 RE-WALK AFTER ESTABLISHING OWNER SESSION (real passwordless-PIN: /login -> emrah.baysal@openova.io -> IMAP BODY.PEEK 6-digit code -> /login/verify -> kcBrokerURL one-time identity-broker roundtrip completed -> realm session on auth.hw292.omani.works; whoami 200 tier=owner) -- the precondition the 2026-08-03T22:10-22:22Z cold-browser walk never established. Refs #3374 #960. Bare https://newapi.hw292.omani.works/ (true first hit, cookie for this host never before set this session) -> lands on /login?expired=true showing a 'Log In' form with a 'Continue with OpenOva SSO' button -- NOT silent, contradicting the row's 'no login page' clause. Clicked the SSO button: no visible navigation within the observed window (screenshot 2 shows the click landed back on the same login page). STILL ❌ on the literal first-hit/no-login-page clause. IMPORTANT ADJACENT FINDING for row 38: a subsequent fresh navigation (after the click) landed fully signed in (see row 38) -- so the OIDC leg does eventually complete, just not instantly/silently on the very first paint the way grafana/gitea/harbor do. Real, distinct defect: newapi's SSO is NOT wired for the same silent auto-redirect the other apps get from the kcBrokerURL roundtrip. SCREENSHOT: [📷 row37-newapi-login-not-signedin.png](../../docs/sessions/2026-08-06/hw292-screenshots/row37-newapi-login-not-signedin.png) (hw292-2026-08-06); SCREENSHOT: [📷 row37-newapi-sso-button-noop.png](../../docs/sessions/2026-08-06/hw292-screenshots/row37-newapi-sso-button-noop.png) (hw292-2026-08-06) ‖ hw292-2026-08-08 — newapi serves its OWN page, it does not redirect to /console. GET https://newapi.hw292.omani.works/ -> HTTP 200 with an EMPTY redirect_url, i.e. no 30x at all. The row's clause is '1st hit lands on /console signed-in'; a 200-with-no-redirect is newapi rendering its own surface instead of handing off. Contrast the five oidc-gate-fronted apps, which 302 to the SSO front door, and contrast bao. which also 302s (row 33). This is the SAME root as #5878/row 38 measured earlier today: newapi is the ONLY SSO app with zero oidc-gate references — it carries a NATIVE Keycloak client (auth.adminUI.mode: keycloak, clientId: newapi-admin), so nothing fronts it to perform the /console handoff. Rows 37 and 38 are therefore ONE defect seen from two directions (first hit vs re-entry), not two. ADDITIONALLY deploy-gated: flux-system/bp-newapi reads Ready=False DependencyNotReady, part of the 51-HR ghcr-pull cascade (#5759), so even the merged newapi work cannot land here. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: DEPLOY-GATED — the fix is MERGED and simply not running on this env. So the five apps that land signed-in off one realm session do so because oidc-gate accepts that session on their behalf; newapi's /login?expired=true is NewAPI's OWN application session, which a Keycloak realm session does not by itself satisfy. 'The only app that rejects the realm session' is really 'the only app whose session is not derived from the thing being tested'. So newapi's FULL OIDC round-trip (authorize → token exchange → user create → bind → promote to admin) HAS completed on this Sovereign. That REFUTES two earlier readings: (a) 'the realm session is rejected by newapi' — that session is what minted the binding; and (b) the redirect_uri defect the chart documents at platform/newapi/chart/values.yaml:567-584, whose symptom is the same /login?expired=true bounce — refuted because `options.ServerAddress` reads the correct absolute https://newapi.hw292.omani.works and an exchange demonstrably succeeded. A 7-input matrix was run on the deployed function: an empty placement array now returns `not-reported` where it previously returned `singleton` — the #5515 fix is in the served bundle. All 6 live Applications match their own spec. CONTROL: the matrix includes inputs that produce every other outcome, so the function discriminates rather than returning one value. ‖ SUPERSEDED PRIOR EVIDENCE, retained as the forensic trail and counted for NOTHING: a predecessor env-2026-07-30: PARTIAL and a NEW CAUSE, previously green. cilium renders "Pattern: singleton" with ZERO target cards and "No placement targets reported yet." - but the two apps whose CR ACTUALLY says spec.placement:singleton (harbor, valkey) render "Pattern: active-active" with TWO PRIMARY cards each. CAUSE: derivePattern fails open - placement.ts:61 returns singleton when primaries==0 and standbys==0, so cilium (which really runs a DaemonSet in both regions) reads as singleton; symmetrically primaries>=2 returns active-active, so harbor/valkey legitimately running in both regions contradict their own CR. Overview renders spec.placement while Topology renders derivePattern(runtime) - two contradictory values on one app. NOT a known issue number; filed as a candidate finding. ‖ 2026-07-31 pre-walk fix-map (code+test, NOT a walk stamp — verdict stays ⚠️ until hw292 walks it): the fails-open cause of the bogus 'Pattern: singleton' with zero target cards is FIXED and delivered — placement.ts:107-108 returns PATTERN_NOT_REPORTED when primaries===0, which makes the singleton return UNREACHABLE without a Primary (it sits after the early return). placement.test.ts 21/21 green incl. a dedicated #5515 block pinning both directions: empty list / garbage roles / standby-only all yield not-reported, while a valid Primary and every legitimate multi-target shape must NOT. Delivery proven by ancestry: 796e587b2 (#5519) is an ancestor of image commit fb41faf. Evidence: github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/5515#issuecomment-5142525776 ‖ 2026-08-01 ROOT-CAUSE FIXED (status UNCHANGED — this row's own assertion is Sovereign-scoped and still unwalked): #5515 derivePattern reported a topology richer than the live one. It read in.Regions (DECLARED wizard specs); liveness lives in the BUILT regions, where buildAbsentRegion emits Clusters:nil for a declared region that never converged. Root cause was a MISSING INPUT, not a mis-ordered switch — no argument carried liveness, so reordering cases could not have fixed it. Fix `509222c6e` moves the derivation below the build loop and threads the built regions in, with an in-flight guard so a still-converging prov keeps reporting its declared shape instead of regressing to "unknown". Confirmed against REAL production data `ebf5b6feb`: live mothership record 2c2d746b578c636b carries request.regions=2 DECLARED (me-east-215-a/-b) vs result.regions=1 LIVE — the exact defect condition, in production. ‖ Reordered so the fresh hw292 measurement leads: it is the sole basis for this verdict, and a reader should not scroll past a dead era to find what the stamp rests on. Foreign env labels removed from the superseded text because a ✅ row may not carry one — the drift guard's rule, kept strict rather than weakened to fit my prose. |

All three shared-pg Continuum CRs report standbyAvailable=true, replicationLagSeconds=0, phase=Healthy. INDEPENDENT CONTROL, taken from Postgres itself rather than from any status field the API might misread: pg_stat_replication on the shared-pg primary shows replicas=3 with states streaming/async, streaming/sync, streaming/potential — so a genuinely caught-up, genuinely streaming standby exists. The CR status keys are also confirmed present: standbyAvailable, replicationLagSeconds, phase, primaryRegion, hotStandbyAbsent, switchoverInProgress, leaseHolder, leaseExpiresAt — note the controller writes replicationLagSeconds, NOT walLagSeconds, and standbyAvailable, NOT replicaPromotable, which is exactly the producer/consumer key mismatch #5601 fixes. Row stays ❌ ONLY because the button lives in the console and hw292 runs catalyst-api fad88bd, which predates PR #5621 — the API still derives promotability from keys no producer writes. This is a delivery gap ([#5640](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/5640)), not a DR defect: the standby the button claims not to have provably exists. LIVE WALK 2026-08-04T09:4xZ (kubectl, both regions) — verdict UNCHANGED at ❌, and the walk shows the row fails for a reason that is NOT the one its wording implies. **The armed-CONDITION is fully satisfied.** A live 2-region shared-pg pair exists and is healthy: region-a `shared-data/shared-pg` instances=3 ready=3 `replica.enabled=false` phase='Cluster in healthy state'; region-b `shared-data/shared-pg-replica` instances=3 ready=3 `replica.enabled=true` phase='Cluster in healthy state'. The Continuum CR backing it also exists and is healthy: `shared-data/dr-shared-pg` phase=Healthy (8 Continuum CRs total on the Sovereign, all Healthy). So the DR substrate this row requires is genuinely there. **What is missing is the single status key the control gates on:** every one of the 8 Continuum CRs reports `status.replicaPromotable` = UNSET. That is #5601 — the reader at `continuum_dr_extras.go` consumes `status.replicaPromotable`, a key the Continuum controller never writes, so the field keeps its Go zero value and the Switchover control reads not-armed while the standby is demonstrably caught up. A regression test already encodes the contradiction (`continuum_dr_extras_5601_test.go:83` — 'the CR says standbyAvailable=true/phase=Healthy/lag=0 and the live replica is Ready'). METHOD NOTE, because it nearly produced a false finding: my first probe used group `continuums.continuum.openova.io` and returned nothing, which reads as 'no Continuum CRs' — the real group is `continuums.dr.openova.io`. A zero from a wrong-group query is indistinguishable from a real absence, so the count above is quoted only after listing the CRD. Row closes when #5601 lands and the control arms against this same live pair — no new DR infrastructure is required.  FIX MERGED 2026-08-06 (#5601, PR #5780): the promotability derivation now consumes status.standbyAvailable + status.phase (the keys the Continuum controller actually writes) instead of status.replicationHealthy, which it never writes — that probe left the zero-value false and disarmed the button over a healthy pair. Mutation-proven both directions. A SECOND unguarded half was found and closed in the same PR: phaseReady accepts Healthy OR FailedOver, and deleting the FailedOver clause left all 8 tests green — so after a switchover the failback control could regress to this row's exact symptom in the one state where an operator cannot recover without it. Deploy-gated on hw292 (pre-fix catalyst-api). ‖ DELIVERY GATE VERIFIED BY ANCESTRY 2026-08-08 (#5640) — verdict unchanged, cause now measured rather than asserted. hw292 runs catalyst-api fad88bd, built 2026-08-02 17:14:57. Every fix PR this row depends on is on origin/main and is NOT an ancestor of that artifact, checked individually with `git merge-base --is-ancestor <merge-sha> fad88bd`: #5621 (b3cbcb359) -> on main YES, in fad88bd NO. The armed-CONDITION is independently proven satisfied (all three shared-pg Continuum CRs standbyAvailable=true, lag 0, phase=Healthy; pg_stat_replication shows 3 streaming replicas), so this is a delivery gap, not a DR defect. So no code work moves this row; the roll (or a fresh prov) does. CLASS FINDING measured the same pass: of the 13 rows at ❌, ALL 13 are DEPLOY-GATED and ZERO are code-blocked (scripts/classify-uat-delivery-state.py --image fad88bd). Writing further fixes moves none of the ❌ column. ‖ hw292-2026-08-08 CAUSE IDENTIFIED — the Switchover button cannot be armed because the object that would arm it DOES NOT EXIST. The row's premise is 'a live 2-region cnpg-pair backs the app'. Measured: CRD cnpgpairs.dr.openova.io IS installed (created 2026-08-03T04:36:57Z) and holds ZERO CRs. Disambiguated the same way G3 was — an empty listing alone cannot tell 'no instances' from 'unknown kind', so the CRD presence was checked explicitly. The Postgres underneath is entirely healthy: shared-pg, shared-pg-b, shared-pg-c all instances=3/readyInstances=3 'Cluster in healthy state', plus cnpg/cnpg-pair-bp-cnpg-pair-primary 3/3 — but that last one is a CNPG *Cluster* NAMED cnpg-pair-*, NOT a CnpgPair CR. The naming makes them easy to conflate; they are different kinds. UNIFIED FINDING with G3: BOTH dr.openova.io CRDs (continuums + cnpgpairs) are installed with zero instances while the underlying Postgres pairs are live. That is exactly the #4986/#4987 shape — a live DR pair with no DR CR above it — and it explains row 57 and G3 with ONE cause rather than two. Stays ❌; the fix belongs to whatever should be creating those CRs. ‖ hw292-2026-08-08 ROOT CAUSE: THERE IS NO PRODUCER. Traced in source, not inferred. Every cnpgpairs reference in the API surface is a READER: k8scache/kinds.go:290 registers it as a cache kind; handler/continuum_dr_extras.go:298 does client.Resource(gvr).Get(...); plus the CRD (chart/crds/cnpgpair.yaml) and the cutover-driver ClusterRole. NOTHING creates one. Same for the sibling: grep -rln 'Continuum{' across core/ and products/ returns ZERO. So the CRD is shipped, RBAC granted, cache wired, reader implemented and continuum-controller deployed 1/1 (5d14h) — with no write path. The consumers were built against CRs nothing emits. NAMING TRAP worth recording: cnpg/cnpg-pair-bp-cnpg-pair-primary is 3/3 healthy, but it is a CNPG *Cluster* NAMED cnpg-pair-*, NOT a CnpgPair CR — different kinds, near-identical names, and reading it as the pairing object makes this row look satisfied when its dependency does not exist. SECOND DEFECT in the same file: runDRPreflight (continuum_dr_extras.go:898) hardcodes preflight-01 continuum-cr-ready=Pass and preflight-02 cnpgpair-streaming=Pass, so on this env the DR preflight reports Pass for CR kinds with ZERO instances — a check that cannot fail on the exact precondition it names, pointed at the DR path. CONSEQUENCE: this row and G3 are ONE defect (a missing producer), not two, and neither is an unrun DR walk. Detail on #4986. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: DEPLOY-GATED — the fix is MERGED and simply not running on this env. Not yet delivered to any env; the runtime pair still needs the hw292 walk. Zero Continuum or CnpgPair objects back this app. THE DEFECT: `status.targets` publishes region-b as `ready: true, standbyType: Hot` while `status.regions` IN THE SAME OBJECT says 0/0 — and TopologyTab renders `targets`, which is exactly why the panel paints a live replica that does not exist. HEALTH HALF PASSES and is sampled per region, not once: grafana pods are 3/3 Running in region-a AND region-b, and a grep over the last 300 lines of all containers returns 'cannot resolve write host' count 0 in region-a and 0 in region-b - no crashloop. CONSOLE HALF IMPROVED: /app/bp-grafana now resolves to the platform component (data-testid app-detail-namespace=flux-system, app-detail-phase=Ready), header and Overview both read STATUS Ready, and the Topology tab enumerates BOTH regions explicitly as live cards - 'hw-me-east-215-a-rtz-prod . 1c56518035a83e03 . mgmt / PRIMARY / serves writes' and 'hw-me-east-215-b-rtz-prod . 1c56518035a83e03-me-east-215-b-1 . mgmt / PRIMARY / serves writes', Pattern: active-active, both cluster ids populated. HELD AT PARTIAL, not upgraded: the row asks for a per-region Healthy/Running verdict and what renders is a single aggregate Ready plus a per-region PLACEMENT ROLE; the dedicated Status panel still reads verbatim 'n/a - bootstrap component (HelmRelease, no Application CR). Live rollout status is tracked via Flux.' - which is exactly the hw291 residual, unchanged. Cause is structural and unfixed on main: bootstrap-kit HRs have no Application CR, so GET .../applications/bp-grafana/status returns 404 application-not-found and there is no per-region health to render. Category (a): no merged-on-main fix found for this leg.  ROOT CAUSE UNIFIED 2026-08-07 (source read, no env needed) — this row's residual is NOT specific to its app, and it is the SAME cause as rows 19/188. The Status panel string the walk quotes verbatim is emitted at products/catalyst/bootstrap/ui/src/pages/sovereign/AppDetail/TopologyTab.tsx:1045-1047, gated on `isBootstrap`: when the component is a bootstrap-kit HelmRelease with no Application CR, the panel renders 'n/a — bootstrap component (HelmRelease, no Application CR). Live rollout status is tracked via Flux.' unconditionally. So the per-region HEALTH verdict this row asks for is not missing because of anything wrong with grafana or keycloak — the data plane measured GREEN in both regions (pods Running per region, zero matching error lines per region). It is missing because the Application-keyed status surface deliberately declines to speak for bootstrap components, which have no Application CR to speak about. ONE CAUSE, FOUR ROWS: 19 and 188 (the apps grid is keyed on the blueprint CATALOG, so no Application CR renders as its own card) and 67 and 69 (the app-detail Status panel abstains for bootstrap components). Both are the same structural fact — bootstrap-kit components are HelmReleases WITHOUT Application CRs, so every Application-keyed surface degrades for exactly that set. Fixing one without the other would move a symptom. NOT PATCHING THIS UNILATERALLY: the honest fix is either (a) synthesise Application CRs for bootstrap components so the whole Application-keyed surface works uniformly, or (b) give the Status panel a HelmRelease-backed per-region health path so it stops abstaining. (a) changes the object model; (b) changes what 'Status' means. Both are founder-level design calls, and the row cannot go green until one is chosen. ‖ THE SINGLE REMAINING RESIDUAL IS FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5835, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292), and it was the SAME defect in rows 67 and 69. The Status panel reading 'n/a - bootstrap component (HelmRelease, no Application CR)' is not a UI copy choice: HandleApplicationStatus read the Application CR AND NOTHING ELSE, so a bootstrap-kit component (an HR with no companion CR) got a hard 404, while its SIBLING GET /applications/{name} returned a complete synthesised answer (synthesiseAppFromHelmRelease, and since #5827 synthesiseAppFromRuntime). The console reacted the only way it could - TopologyTab.tsx:144 DISABLES the poll entirely for isBootstrap - and printed the n/a string. So one page contradicted itself: this row's own walk measured the header and Overview reading STATUS Ready, from the synthesised detail response, while the Status panel three inches below said there was no status to report. The data existed the whole time; only that endpoint refused to project it. FIX: /status gets the same fallback chain as the detail endpoint, HR first then runtime. It DELEGATES rather than re-deriving - computing a phase from the HelmRelease here would be a THIRD derivation of one component's status, and two was already enough for the detail and status paths to disagree in front of an operator. ORDERING IS GUARDED: runtime-first would reduce a bootstrap component's declared identity to observed Pods on this endpoint while the detail endpoint still reports it - the same disagreement in the other direction. No spec is invented (no CR means no spec.placement; the Topology tab derives real placement from live Pods via .../placement, #3982). 4 guards, mutation-verified: delete the call site; drop the runtime fall-through; flip the ordering. A CLAIM I RETRACTED BEFORE SHIPPING: my first conditions test asserted the key is always PRESENT on the wire. The struct tag says otherwise - conditions carries omitempty, so an empty slice is omitted entirely, on the CR-backed path as much as this one. Callers tolerate an absent key; what they cannot tolerate is null where a list is expected. The stronger assertion would have pinned a contract the existing code does not honour and broken the CR path this change does not touch. NOT CLAIMED: the FE isBootstrap gate is NOT removed - that is a separate change and should follow a live re-walk confirming the endpoint now answers. This row stays partial until then. Gates: go vet clean, full handler suite rc=0 254s; noted honestly, an immediately prior identical run reported FAIL at 253s with no capturable failure lines, so there is an unidentified flake in the suite and this is ONE clean run, not a verified-stable claim. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 PROMOTED ⚠️ → ❌ — deploy-gated on the API half, but a REAL FRONT-END WALL remains that a roll cannot clear. #5836 makes `/status` answer, yet `TopologyTab.tsx:144` still disables the poll when `isBootstrap`, and `:1045-1047` HARD-RENDERS the literal `n/a — bootstrap component` string regardless of what the API returns. Both lines are still on main today. So the row needs the front-end un-gated, not merely the image rolled. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: DEPLOY-GATED — the fix is MERGED and simply not running on this env. Re-walking here reproduces this same FAIL; it closes on a roll or a fresh prov, not on more measurement.|

So one page contradicted itself: this row's own walk measured the header and Overview reading STATUS Ready, from the synthesised detail response, while the Status panel three inches below said there was no status to report. The data existed the whole time; only that endpoint refused to project it. FIX: /status gets the same fallback chain as the detail endpoint, HR first then runtime. It DELEGATES rather than re-deriving - computing a phase from the HelmRelease here would be a THIRD derivation of one component's status, and two was already enough for the detail and status paths to disagree in front of an operator. ORDERING IS GUARDED: runtime-first would reduce a bootstrap component's declared identity to observed Pods on this endpoint while the detail endpoint still reports it - the same disagreement in the other direction. No spec is invented (no CR means no spec.placement; the Topology tab derives real placement from live Pods via .../placement, #3982). 4 guards, mutation-verified: delete the call site; drop the runtime fall-through; flip the ordering. A CLAIM I RETRACTED BEFORE SHIPPING: my first conditions test asserted the key is always PRESENT on the wire. The struct tag says otherwise - conditions carries omitempty, so an empty slice is omitted entirely, on the CR-backed path as much as this one. Callers tolerate an absent key; what they cannot tolerate is null where a list is expected. The stronger assertion would have pinned a contract the existing code does not honour and broken the CR path this change does not touch. NOT CLAIMED: the FE isBootstrap gate is NOT removed - that is a separate change and should follow a live re-walk confirming the endpoint now answers. This row stays partial until then. Gates: go vet clean, full handler suite rc=0 254s; noted honestly, an immediately prior identical run reported FAIL at 253s with no capturable failure lines, so there is an unidentified flake in the suite and this is ONE clean run, not a verified-stable claim. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 PROMOTED ⚠️ → ❌ — identical cause to row 67 (`TopologyTab.tsx:144` poll gate + `:1045-1047` hard-rendered n/a string, both still on main). Recorded separately so neither row is closed by a fix that only addresses the other. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: DEPLOY-GATED — the fix is MERGED and simply not running on this env. Re-walking here reproduces this same FAIL; it closes on a roll or a fresh prov, not on more measurement.|

| 72 | funnel | [#3376](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/3376) | Operator console BSS → Vouchers page renders the voucher issuance form (code, credit OMR, plan tier, description); no login wall. | hw292 | ✅ | hw292-2026-08-03T06:48Z AUTHED OWNER WALK (dep 1c56518035a83e03, PIN session): PASS — BSS Billing → Vouchers renders and the "+ Issue voucher" CTA opens the full issuance form: Code field (optional; blank auto-generates; inline hint "a custom code needs >= 12 characters with >= 6 distinct"), Credit (OMR) spinbutton, Plan-tier select with real pricing (S 5 / M 9 / L 16 / XL 30 / Flexi 0 OMR/mo). FINDING (cosmetic, #5123-B class): this billing sub-app header renders a "Sign in" chip + generic "U User" identity while the owner controls still function — session-scope display gap, recorded not fixed SCREENSHOT: [📷 row72-bss-voucher-form.png](../../docs/sessions/2026-08-06/hw292-screenshots/row72-bss-voucher-form.png) (hw292-2026-08-06)  ‖ THE SIGN-IN-BUTTON RESIDUAL IS FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5825, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292), and it was the SAME defect in rows 1, 26 and 72 - one cause, three rows. ProfileMenu decided 'signed in' from useSession ALONE (the cookie session off GET /whoami). The sovereign console can also be authenticated by the legacy OIDC PKCE token set, where /whoami answers 401 and session.signedIn is false. SovereignSidebar ALREADY falls back to the id_token claims for exactly this reason (#4187, its own comment says so) - so the two identity readers in the SAME header disagreed: the sidebar painted 'EB emrah.baysal@openova.io' while ProfileMenu painted a [Sign in] button three inches away. That is precisely what this row recorded. NOT COSMETIC: the row's clause is 'lands /dashboard signed-in as the owner (no PIN/login form)', and a visible sign-in affordance IS a login form by that clause's own definition - which is why the row sat at partial rather than green. FIX: ProfileMenu applies the sidebar's fallback chain with the sidebar's precedence - cookie authoritative, id_token fallback - so one resolved identity drives the avatar, its title and the dropdown and the two readers cannot name different people. EXPIRY IS CHECKED, not just presence: a stale token set outlives the session in sessionStorage, and accepting it would trade a false 'signed out' for a false 'signed in', the worse direction, because it fronts an avatar for a session that can no longer call the API. Uses isTokenExpired, the same 60s-margin helper the OIDC refresh path uses, so it cannot drift from that layer. sessionStorage access is wrapped - privacy mode throws and the header must never crash over an optional fallback. 5 guards, and 4 mutations each turned a DIFFERENT one red with no overlap: session-only revert, dropped expiry check, sub-as-email fallback, precedence flip. The storage-key constants are duplicated in the fixture, which is a real hazard - a typo makes loadTokens() return null and every 'rejects' assertion pass for the wrong reason - so the POSITIVE test is what keeps them honest. Gates: tsc rc=0, 4 auth+layout suites 24 tests green.|

CONTROL PROVING IT IS THE CHECKOUT LEG, NOT MAIL: the CONSOLE PIN path delivered to this same mailbox 90 min earlier in this session — 3 independent codes, same IMAP reader, each verify 200 + whoami 200 (row 235, green today). Mail works, mailbox works, console issuance works; marketplace checkout issuance does not. The marketplace pin/issue API returns 200 and the UI reacts to that success, so the failure is AFTER the API answers and is silent. Traced my own click in the gateway access log: '10:05:39 POST /auth/magic-link status=200 duration=3.794s'. That ~3.8s is SMTP-shaped latency, so the handler attempts a send, takes ~4s, and reports success regardless. NOT a never-worked: the same log holds a COMPLETE successful chain two days earlier — 2026-08-07 22:58:40 magic-link 200 (3.76s) -> 22:58:48 /auth/verify 200 -> /auth/me 200. A code was issued, delivered, read and accepted on this same Sovereign. WORKING 2026-08-07 22:58, BROKEN 2026-08-09 10:05. Next look: catalyst-system/marketplace-api holds SMTP_HOST/PORT/USER/PASS + FROM_EMAIL, all via valueFrom not literals, and its logs contain ZERO mail/smtp lines in 300 entries despite a 3.8s send — so either the secret those resolve from is empty/stale, or the send error is swallowed. The swallowed-error shape is worse: an issuance path returning 200 on a failed send looks healthy to every monitor while no customer can ever buy. ‖ hw292-2026-08-09 ROOT CAUSE IS A SOVEREIGNTY TETHER (#5921). marketplace-api sends the customer sign-in code via smtp-host=mail.openova.io:587, resolved from Secret/sovereign-smtp-credentials with all five keys present and non-empty — so it is wired deliberately, not config drift. hw292 is POST-CUTOVER with cutoverComplete=true, meaning a Sovereign asserted independent of the OpenOva mothership still needs OUR mail server for every customer sign-in. ADR-0002 / DOD Pillar 5 enumerate EIGHT tethers that cutover pivots; outbound customer-auth SMTP is not among them, so no step pivots it and no check looks for it. Step-08's deny-egress proof holds github.com / ghcr.io / harbor.openova.io for 600s — mail.openova.io is not in that set and no reconcile needs SMTP, so the hold passes with this tether fully live. Same shape as the 62 ghcr.io HelmRepositories that survived the same proof (#5919). NOT ISOLATED: a CiliumNetworkPolicy/baseline-default-deny is in force and egress denial to :587 is consistent with everything measured, but the pod is distroless so I could not dial from inside it; the alternative is a swallowed send error. Either way the tether is the thing that should not exist, and it materially qualifies the Pillar-5 green. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: NEEDS-CODE — no fix exists yet. This row is engineering work, not a walk; a re-walk cannot change it.|

Filed #5894. (2) THE FIRST PAINT IS BLANK: a cold browser reaching the console 200 is redirected to the Org's own keycloak with prompt=none and then STOPS THERE with body_len=0 and an empty title - still identical after a 25s wait, so terminal rather than mid-redirect. A RELOAD recovers to console.../login?next=%2Fdashboard, title 'OpenOva Corporate', 1 email input. So the silent-auth response is unhandled and the customer's first visit to their new console is a blank page. Filed #5895. The row asks for an IDENTICAL ZERO-CLICK CONTRACT; a coin-flip connection followed by a blank page that needs a manual reload is not that. Evidence docs/sessions/2026-08-08/hw292-funnel/hw292-org2-console-blank.png. ROOT CAUSE NOW KNOWN, and it supersedes the 'unhandled prompt=none response' wording above: the console is navigating to an IdP host that DOES NOT EXIST. detectMode.ts:38-51 derives the Sovereign FQDN by slicing the leading 'console.' off window.location.hostname, so console.walk-stranger-two.omani.rest yields walk-stranger-two.omani.rest; router.tsx:390 reads that into DETECTED_MODE.sovereignFQDN, router.tsx:415 passes it to initiateLogin(fqdn,{prompt:'none'}), and oidc.ts:56 builds https://auth.${sovereignFQDN}/realms/sovereign. Measured, same path and query on each host: auth.hw292.omani.works -> 400 (Keycloak present, rejecting a deliberately bogus redirect_uri), auth.uatco.omani.homes -> 404, auth.walk-stranger-two.omani.rest -> 404 (host root and /.well-known/openid-configuration also 404). The 400 is the control that makes it conclusive. There is NO per-Org IdP by design - gitops.go:307 builds the single shared issuer at auth.<SovereignFQDN>/realms/<realm> (#4272, #4399) - so the heuristic's assumption that every console.* host is a Sovereign is what breaks. The browser gets 404 with a zero-byte body and paints nothing. AND THE RELOAD 'RECOVERY' IS ALSO EXPLAINED: router.tsx sets SILENT_SSO_GUARD_KEY in sessionStorage BEFORE navigating, so the second load short-circuits to /login - the loop-guard is what recovers, not the silent path. A tripwire test is merged (#5899, detectMode.test.ts) that goes RED the day this is fixed. ‖ CAUSE IDENTIFIED 2026-08-08 (stays ❌ — reproducibly fails, but is NOT an independent defect). 10 fresh connections per host: console.uatco.omani.homes 200=5/reset=5; console.walk-stranger-two.omani.rest 200=5/reset=5; CONTROL console.hw292.omani.works 200=10/reset=0 on the SAME IP. Isolation, each step ruling one thing out: DNS identical (both per-Org hosts resolve 212.72.24.85, same as the working sovereign host) so not DNS; `openssl s_client` CONNECTs to both so not a handshake incapability; the real curl error is `(35) SSL_connect: Connection reset by peer` — reset MID-HANDSHAKE, not a cert rejection. METHOD NOTE: a single probe returned 000 for uatco and 200 for walk-stranger-two, which invited 'uatco is broken, the other is fine' — ten probes each show BOTH at exactly 50%, so one probe would have produced a confidently wrong per-host verdict. Exactly-50% on both pool-TLD hosts with 0% on the sovereign host reproduces the #5894 measurement, whose root cause was traced the same day: region B carries ZERO per-Org Gateway listeners because orgConsoleTLSTargets fans out host-region-first and the catalyst-api OOM truncates it before region B (#5642). Fix is #5645 — MERGED, never executed, gated behind #5640. Round-robin lands on the listener-less region ~half the time and that region resets. Row 94 therefore joins the deploy-gated set; no code needs writing for it. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: DEPLOY-GATED — the fix is MERGED and simply not running on this env. Re-walking here reproduces this same FAIL; it closes on a roll or a fresh prov, not on more measurement.|

Reset to ☐ deliberately: a new clause has never been walked, and inheriting the old row's verdict would be the fabrication this ledger exists to stop. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 FIRST WALK OF THE REPLACEMENT CLAUSE. NOT WALKABLE READ-ONLY, and I will not fake it. Deleting an Organization is a write, and every walk in this pass was read-only by construction. WHAT WOULD CLOSE IT, precisely: delete an M-plan Org, then observe its `sts vcluster` and namespace garbage-collected with no orphan left behind. That is a mutation walk on a throwaway Org, not something to infer from the delete-cascade code. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 NOT CLOSED - THE ONLY AVAILABLE SUBJECT MAKES THE CLAUSE VACUOUS, stated rather than banked. The one deleted Organization on this env is r17probe. Zero remnants survive it: 0 StatefulSets, 0 namespaces, 0 Services, 0 PVCs, 0 HelmReleases match the name. That LOOKS like a clean cascade and it is not evidence of one. Its catalyst-api record shows steps.vcluster=`pending` with created_at 2026-08-03T18:16:56Z and updated_at 18:17:38Z - deleted 42 seconds after creation, before the vcluster leg ever ran. There was never a vCluster to orphan, so `no orphaned vCluster survives` is true for a reason that has nothing to do with the delete cascade. CONTROL CONFIRMING THE QUERY DISCRIMINATES: the two live Organizations uatco and walk-stranger-two both DO retain sts/vcluster 1/1, so the census is not returning zero because it cannot see StatefulSets. WHAT CLOSES THIS ROW: delete an Organization whose steps.vcluster reached `done`, then re-run the same census. Recording the vacuity instead of the green is the point - a null proven against a subject that never existed is the failure mode this ledger exists to catch. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 WALKED LIVE - PASS, on a subject that makes the assertion non-vacuous. SUBJECT CHOSEN DELIBERATELY: Organization walk-stranger-two, whose record reads steps.vcluster=`done` and state=`done` - unlike r17probe (steps.vcluster=`pending`, deleted 42s after creation), which is why the earlier attempt could not close this row. BASELINE CAPTURED BEFORE THE MUTATION (docs/sessions/2026-08-10/evidence/row107-baseline-before-delete.txt): ns walk-stranger-two Active, statefulset.apps/vcluster 1/1 at 47h, 3 bound PVCs including data-vcluster-0. The thing that must not be orphaned demonstrably existed. ACTION: deleted the Organization CR, which the catalyst-api source itself names as the real teardown trigger - organization_provisioning.go:1023 comment on #5426 says marking the CR fires the `orgs.openova.io/tenant-networking` finalizer cascade (namespace, vCluster, Keycloak realm, per-Org Flux sources, gateway listeners, certs). The CR carried exactly that finalizer before deletion. RESULT (docs/sessions/2026-08-10/evidence/row107-after-delete-cascade.txt): the vcluster StatefulSet was GONE within 20 seconds and the namespace finished Terminating by t+80s. At t+320s: zero walk-stranger-two remnants across ns, sts, deploy, svc, pvc and helmrelease, and the Organization CR is NotFound. CONTROL: uatco/vcluster is STILL 1/1 after the cascade, so the census discriminates rather than returning zero because it stopped seeing StatefulSets at all. MEASUREMENT CORRECTION RECORDED RATHER THAN HIDDEN: my polling loop printed CR=1 for 300s and I briefly read it as a stuck finalizer. It was my own bug - the loop piped `kubectl get ... 2>&1` into grep, and kubectl's NotFound error text CONTAINS the string walk-stranger-two, so the grep counted the error as a hit. A direct jsonpath query returns NotFound and the finalizer completed normally. A phantom defect filed off a merged-stderr grep is precisely the failure mode this ledger exists to catch. SEPARATE REAL FINDING, filed rather than folded in: GET /api/v1/organizations/walk-stranger-two returns 200 while DELETE on the SAME path returns 404 org-tenant-not-found, and DELETE by org_tenant_id 404s too - read and delete resolve identifiers differently, so the console's own delete path could not perform this teardown. That is why the CR path was used. |

Same two blockers as row 35, measured in the same pass: the console PIN wall (#5642), and guacamole_connection = 0 rows so the connections list would be empty even with a session. The admin grants ARE present — sovereign-admins holds ADMINISTER and five CREATE_* permissions in guacamole_system_permission — so this row is not an authorization defect. Connection seeding is the gap, and per the #5598 investigation that seeding is non-trivial: guacd's kubernetes protocol cannot produce bp-k8s-ws-proxy's HMAC-signed upgrade headers, so a naively seeded connection would fail on click.  ROOT CAUSE 2026-08-06: shares row 35's enrollment race (#5598) for the permission half — see that row for the mechanism and the falsifier. The guacamole_connection = 0 clause is separate and unaddressed by it. ‖ hw292-2026-08-08 — CORROBORATES row 35 and adds the blocking dependency. Guacamole SERVES and the SSO shape is right: https://guacamole.hw292.omani.works/ returns 302 on 4/4 fresh connections — a redirect, NOT the 200-with-a-form that this row's 'no Guacamole login form' clause forbids. Pods are all up (guacamole-server, guacd, guacamole-pg all Running; the admin-enroll CronJob Completed 5s before the reading). BUT the release is STALE and now we know why: HelmRelease conditions read BY TYPE — Ready=False DependencyNotReady 'dependency flux-system/bp-cilium is not ready'; Released=True UpgradeSucceeded '... chart bp-guacamole@0.2.33'; desired=0.2.37 applied=0.2.33. So the 0.2.36 crossRegion-singleton fix (#5358) is still NOT applied, exactly as row 35 recorded, and the upgrade is held by a bp-cilium dependency rather than by the chart. METHOD CORRECTION worth recording: my first read used spec.chart.spec.version (0.2.37) and conditions[0].status, and concluded 'Ready=True on 0.2.37'. Both were wrong — spec.chart.spec.version is the DESIRED version (status.history[0].chartVersion is what is applied), and conditions[0] is Reconciling, not Ready. A CONTROL on a known-good HR confirmed the by-type query returns Ready=True where it should. Desired-vs-applied and condition-by-index are two distinct ways to read a stale release as healthy. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: NEEDS-CODE — no fix exists yet. This row is engineering work, not a walk; a re-walk cannot change it.|

Reproduced 3/3 loads. DOWNGRADED ✅→⚠️: the no-token-form SSO-landing half is proven, the engine-listing half regressed since 2026-08-04 (real finding, not a walker error). SCREENSHOT: [📷 row183-openbao-bare-url-notauthorized.png](../../docs/sessions/2026-08-06/hw292-screenshots/row183-openbao-bare-url-notauthorized.png) (hw292-2026-08-06)  ‖ CANDIDATE ROOT CAUSE FOUND + FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5829, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292), and the 08-04-PASS / 08-06-FAIL split on the SAME env is what pointed at it. In the sso-configure reconcile loop the two sys/policies/acl writes were the ONLY writes whose failure was swallowed - `if bao_write ...; then log ...; fi` with NO else - while the auth/oidc/role/operator write SIX LINES BELOW already did `else log WARN; continue`. So a failed policy write did not stop the tick: the role was then written with token_policies=[sso-operator-read], naming a policy that does not exist. OpenBao accepts that role; its tokens carry a dangling policy name and therefore NO capabilities, and every request 403s - including sys/internal/ui/mounts, which is exactly what the UI calls to fill the Secrets Engines panel. That is this row's symptom verbatim: session genuine (final URL /ui/vault/secrets, hasTokenInput=false, identity badge present) while the panel reads 'Not authorized', 3/3 loads, with NO error anywhere because the LOGIN SUCCEEDS. It also explains the date split: the 08-04 walk hit the bare host with no prior session, the 08-06 walk was explicitly POST-SESSION-ESTABLISH, so only the second exercised the OIDC-minted token rather than the bootstrap one. FIX: both policy writes now gate the tick. `continue`, NOT a louder log - the failure mode is ORDERING, a policy must EXIST before a role names it, and writing the role first with the policy 'next tick' leaves a window where the operator is authenticated and powerless. Guard tests/sso-policy-write-gates-tick-5829.sh, mutation-verified (reverting EITHER gate turns it red); it asserts on `continue` INSIDE the extracted write block rather than on the file containing `continue` anywhere (the render has several - a whole-file grep could never go red), opens with a vacuity control proving the script rendered at all, and pins the policy-before-role ORDERING separately because if the role write moves above the policy writes every `continue` becomes decorative. Lockstep 5 sites, bp-openbao 1.2.65->1.2.66 + bp-catalyst-platform 1.4.1329->1.4.1330; check-bootstrap-kit-pin-sync 67 pairs PASS, catalog-seed lockstep 68 checked 0 fail, g117-5-sso-tier1 still PASS. HONEST SCOPE: I could NOT reach hw292's OpenBao to confirm the live token's policy set. The walk's own evidence is consistent with this cause and I found no other path that produces a 403 on that endpoint from a cleanly authenticated session, but this is a SOURCE-DERIVED diagnosis, not a live one, and the row stays partial until re-walked. The fix is unconditionally correct either way: a swallowed write that leaves a role referencing a missing policy is a defect on its own terms. ‖ NO-PARTIALS DISPOSITION 2026-08-10 — RECLASSIFIED ⚠️ -> ❌. Every clause in a test case must hold for the row to pass; this row's evidence NAMES a clause that does not. A partial is a FAILING test case with some passing parts, and carrying it as its own class let real failures sit in a comfort bucket where no backlog counted them — 27 rows were doing exactly that. NOTHING WAS RE-MEASURED HERE and no verdict got worse: the residual named above is the same residual, in the same words, with the same evidence. Only the class changed, from one that reads as 'nearly there' to one that reads as 'does not pass'. This moves ZERO rows out of green, so it is neither progress nor regression — it is the removal of a hiding place. The row closes when the named residual closes. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: DEPLOY-GATED — the fix is MERGED and simply not running on this env. Re-walking here reproduces this same FAIL; it closes on a roll or a fresh prov, not on more measurement.|
