BACKEND, right for the right reason: GET .../applications/catalyst-api/placement returns exactly 1 target {hw-me-east-215-a-rtz-prod, cluster 1c56518035a83e03, Primary} with derivedFromRuntime:true - same 1-target answer for catalyst-ui and marketplace-api - and the runtime cross-check agrees: catalyst-system in region-a runs 11 Deployments including catalyst-api 1/1, while region-b's catalyst-system contains ONLY openova-flow-server, so singleton is the correct answer. UI LEG UNCHANGED: https://console.hw292.omani.works/app/catalyst-api renders 'App not found - The component catalyst-api is not part of this deployment', so the row's own subject still cannot be walked in a browser. NEAREST-PROXY DEFECT RESOLVED: on hw290, /app/bp-catalyst-platform Topology claimed 'Pattern: active-hot-standby' from a single malformed Standby target - the exact false-multi-region this row exists to catch. On hw292 it no longer makes that claim: Overview PLACEMENT reads 'singleton' and the Topology tab reads 'Pattern: not reported' with an explicit honesty line - 'This placement DECLARES a standby, but the replication endpoint returned a fallback reading (source: pending) - no live Continuum / cnpg pair backs it.' RESIDUAL, and it is the same data as hw290 minus the false label: the Placement strip is still sourced from the SUSPENDED region - the only card is 'hw-me-east-215-b-rtz-prod . . mgmt / STANDBY . Hot' with an empty cluster id, while region-b's HR is spec.suspend=true and region-a's is suspend=false Ready=True (chart bp-catalyst-platform@1.4.1264) - so the live PRIMARY card is absent and the render comes from the suspended leg. Stays PARTIAL on the UI leg only; no false multi-region remains. ‖ screenshots docs/sessions/2026-08-06/hw292-uat-rewalk-14rows/hw292-row188-catalyst-api-not-found.png + hw292-row188-bp-catalyst-platform-topology.png  ROOT CAUSE SHARPENED 2026-08-07 (source read, no env needed) — the residual is ARCHITECTURAL, not a count bug. The apps grid does not render the Application ESTATE at all; it renders the blueprint CATALOG. AppsPage.tsx:116 builds its card list from `resolveApplications(store.selectedComponents)`, and applicationCatalog.ts:72 iterates BOOTSTRAP_KIT plus the selected component set — i.e. the wizard store's catalog. Application CRs never contribute card IDENTITY. They are layered in only as STATUS: AppsPage.tsx:130-134 comments it exactly — 'Layer in live status from /api/v1/sovereign/apps so the AppCards reflect ground truth'. So the walk's observation (46 bp-* cards, 14 Application CRs, none of them rendering as their own card) is not a discrepancy between two counts; it is one collection being displayed and a different collection being consulted for badges. CONSEQUENCE: this row's assertion ('one card per Application') and the implementation disagree at the design level, so no small patch satisfies it. Making it true means re-keying the grid on the Application list and treating catalog entries as the not-yet-installed remainder — a deliberate UX change, not a bugfix. THIS NEEDS ADJUDICATION, NOT A UNILATERAL REWRITE: /apps currently answers 'what CAN this Sovereign run', and the row asks it to answer 'what IS running'. Both are legitimate products; only the founder should choose. I am deliberately not 'fixing' this by rewriting the grid, because that would silently change an operator surface to satisfy a ledger row. Sibling: row 188 (catalyst-api likewise has no card) is the same cause. ‖ THE UI LEG IS FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5827, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292). This row's own evidence contained the contradiction and it took a source read to see it: GET .../applications/catalyst-api/placement answers 200 with a correct single Primary target (this row measured that), while GET .../applications/catalyst-api answers 404 - two endpoints on the SAME path prefix disagreeing about whether the component exists. Cause: catalyst-api is a Deployment rendered BY the bp-catalyst-platform HR, so it owns neither an Application CR nor a same-named HelmRelease and HandleApplicationGet's two lookups both miss; the placement endpoint does not care because it derives from LIVE PODS. So the API simultaneously knew the component and denied it existed depending on the suffix, and the console rendered 'App not found - the component catalyst-api is not part of this deployment' for something plainly running in front of the caller. That is exactly why this row's own subject could not be walked in a browser while its backend clause passed. FIX: a THIRD and last fallback, synthesiseAppFromRuntime, after the CR and the HR both miss. ONE IDENTITY PREDICATE, NOT TWO - existence is decided by podBelongsToComponent, the exact function the placement path uses; re-answering the same question a second way IS the defect, not an implementation detail of it. Readiness likewise reuses the existing podIsReady (I wrote one, found the identical function in dashboard.go, deleted mine). FABRICATES NOTHING: no CR means no uid/blueprint/version/parameters/environmentRef, all left empty rather than guessed; runtimeDerived:true marks the whole answer as an OBSERVATION so a consumer can tell 'declares nothing' from 'declaration not fetched' without inferring it from which fields are blank. Phase is strict - Ready requires EVERY matched Pod ready, because rounding one degraded replica of three up to Ready is how a status panel starts lying. ORDERING IS LOAD-BEARING and guarded: the runtime path is LAST, because running it before the HR lookup would strip every bootstrap component's declared blueprint down to the bare observed shape. 4 guards, mutation-verified. ONE CAUGHT ITSELF and is worth recording: the first omitempty mutation SILENTLY DID NOT APPLY - gofmt had realigned the struct field so the replace matched nothing - and the test 'passed'. A mutation that does not apply is not a passing test, it is an unexecuted mutation. Redone against the real text; it then went red as intended. Gates: go vet clean, full handler suite 254s green. NOT FIXED HERE: this does not give bootstrap components Application CRs. Rows 19, 67 and 69 all trace to that same structural absence and are untouched. ‖ NO-PARTIALS DISPOSITION 2026-08-10 — RECLASSIFIED ⚠️ -> ❌. Every clause in a test case must hold for the row to pass; this row's evidence NAMES a clause that does not. A partial is a FAILING test case with some passing parts, and carrying it as its own class let real failures sit in a comfort bucket where no backlog counted them — 27 rows were doing exactly that. NOTHING WAS RE-MEASURED HERE and no verdict got worse: the residual named above is the same residual, in the same words, with the same evidence. Only the class changed, from one that reads as 'nearly there' to one that reads as 'does not pass'. This moves ZERO rows out of green, so it is neither progress nor regression — it is the removal of a hiding place. The row closes when the named residual closes. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: DEPLOY-GATED — the fix is MERGED and simply not running on this env. Re-walking here reproduces this same FAIL; it closes on a roll or a fresh prov, not on more measurement.|

REVISED 2026-08-03T21:53Z: the #5617 mechanism cited here is REFUTED on this env, so this row's remaining failure is NOT a Cilium egress denial. hubble observe --namespace uatco --verdict DROPPED --last 200 on cilium-cfspn (the agent on the node the gate pod actually runs on) returns ZERO dropped flows, with a CONTROL on the same agent and namespace showing live FORWARDED traffic — so the empty result is a real absence of drops, not a blind probe. The namespace also carries allow-gateway-and-apiserver with toEndpoints:[{}], an empty selector matching every endpoint incl. kube-dns. PR #5624 is still worth having (a workload should declare its own egress rather than lean on a broad namespace-wide allow), but it must not be described as fixing a DNS timeout this env does not exhibit. What remains unproven is the OAuth callback itself: only kube-probe health checks traversed the gate in the observed window. ROOT CAUSE NAMED 2026-08-04T05:2xZ (structural proof, deployed binary — verdict unchanged at ❌): the failure is #5617 and it is measurable without a browser. The constraining object in ns `uatco` is CiliumNetworkPolicy `allow-gateway-and-apiserver` with `endpointSelector: {}` — it selects EVERY pod in the Org namespace and declares an egress section, which under Cilium makes egress deny-by-default for all of them. It carries exactly 2 egress rules: `toEntities:[kube-apiserver]` on 443/6443, and `toEndpoints:[{}]` (same namespace). **Port-53 rules present: 0.** So cluster DNS is denied namespace-wide, the oidc-gate cannot resolve `keycloak.keycloak.svc.cluster.local`, and the OAuth callback 500s after a successful login. VACUITY CONTROL for the probe: the identical jq over all CiliumNetworkPolicies finds **5 policies that DO carry port-53 egress** (e.g. `catalyst-system/baseline-default-deny` dns_rules=2, `catalyst-system/openova-mcp-bp-openova-mcp-egress` dns_rules=2, `cnpg/cnpg-pair-bp-cnpg-pair-dr-failback-egress` dns_rules=2) — so a 0 for uatco is a real absence, not a probe that returns 0 for everything. Fix merged today as PR #5653 (per-Org baseline CNP gains 53/UDP+TCP to kube-system/kube-dns at L3/L4, plus bp-oidc-gate 0.1.9 shipping its own policy pair, plus `scripts/check-netpol-egress-completeness.py` as a fail-closed class gate over 36 charts / 109 policy documents). NOT yet delivered to this env — per #5640 no published image reaches a cutoverComplete=true Sovereign, so this row stays ❌ until a fresh prov or a side-load carries it. ‖ DELIVERY GATE VERIFIED BY ANCESTRY 2026-08-08 (#5640) — verdict unchanged, cause now measured rather than asserted. hw292 runs catalyst-api fad88bd, built 2026-08-02 17:14:57. Every fix PR this row depends on is on origin/main and is NOT an ancestor of that artifact, checked individually with `git merge-base --is-ancestor <merge-sha> fad88bd`: #5647 (e5fc89203), #5713 (de754137a), #5723 (10ab5c70b), #5732 (7c4eca302) -> all on main YES, all in fad88bd NO. The convergence half of this row already passes on every clause; it fails only on the per-Org host flapping whose class fix is exactly those four PRs. So no code work moves this row; the roll (or a fresh prov) does. CLASS FINDING measured the same pass: of the 13 rows at ❌, ALL 13 are DEPLOY-GATED and ZERO are code-blocked (scripts/classify-uat-delivery-state.py --image fad88bd). Writing further fixes moves none of the ❌ column. ‖ hw292-2026-08-08 — the per-Org Agenity workspace leg is UP, the newapi leg is NOT, and the difference is instructive. uatco/uatco-agenity-rtz-a reads Ready=True InstallSucceeded, so the per-Org agenity workspace this journey depends on did install into a customer Org. But flux-system/bp-newapi reads Ready=False DependencyNotReady — one of the 51 HelmReleases blocked by the ghcr-pull credential cascade traced today (#5759: bp-cilium SourceNotReady, 'no auth config for ghcr.io in docker-registry Secret ghcr-pull', fanning out through bp-cert-manager/bp-cilium/bp-cnpg/bp-flux/bp-keycloak). So this end-to-end journey cannot complete on hw292 for an infrastructure reason, not a journey-logic one: the Org-scoped half works and a platform-scoped dependency is frozen. Stays ❌, attributed to the delivery gate. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: DEPLOY-GATED — the fix is MERGED and simply not running on this env. Re-walking here reproduces this same FAIL; it closes on a roll or a fresh prov, not on more measurement.|
